I wouldn’t say I’m down exactly. I mean, I have slow days like anybody, especially when the weather’s bad—rain messes with my knees a bit, and then I end up watching those old house repair shows, which always make everything look easier than it is.

Not like a sudden mood thing, no. It’s more… since I stopped working, I just don’t seem to have an off switch. I’m up and moving all day, going from one little job to the next like somebody’s waiting on me to finish.

I’ll start with, say, tightening something in the kitchen, then I remember the garage shelf is crooked, then I’m out there moving boxes around—actually I found an old coffee can full of screws the other day, from back when I was doing more work with my hands—and before I know it, the whole morning’s gone.

So I don’t know if I’d call it “wired,” exactly. It’s just hard for me to slow down.

I don’t think I’m madder than usual or anything, but since I stopped working, I just can’t seem to slow down.\n\nI’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me or a foreman checking the clock. I don’t know if I’d call it “really high,” exactly. It’s not like I’m celebrating or feeling on top of the world.

It’s more that since I stopped working, I just can’t seem to slow down. I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. I wouldn’t say “up” like in a happy way, or clearly different mood-wise. Maybe on edge, but more like I can’t settle.

Four days or more… I don’t know if I’d put it like “upbeat” or something. It’s not like I suddenly feel fantastic.

What brings me in is more that since I stopped working, I just can’t seem to slow down. I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. I’ll see something that needs fixing, then that turns into another thing, and then I’m halfway into some project I didn’t even mean to start.

It’s just hard for me to slow down.\n\nDoctor: Have there been a time or times when you felt really high, unusually energetic, or extremely productive for several days in a row—like you didn’t need to rest or couldn’t slow down, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “really high,” exactly. It’s not like I’m celebrating or feeling on top of the world.\n\nIt’s more that since I stopped working, I just can’t seem to slow down. I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. I’ll finish one thing and then spot another, and then another, and before long I haven’t really sat down much at all. So… energetic, I guess, but not in a special burst for a few days. More like I’ve still got work mode stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt your mood was not just restless, but clearly elevated or unusually irritable—like you were on edge in a different way, maybe more easily annoyed or unusually 'up'—along with feeling driven to keep going and doing things more than usual, even for a few days in a row?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “up” like in a happy way, or clearly different mood-wise. Maybe on edge, but more like I can’t settle.\n\nWhat brings me in is really that since I stopped working, I can’t seem to slow down. I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. I’ll start doing one thing, then I notice something else that needs doing, and pretty soon I’ve been at it for hours.\n\nIt’s not so much that I’m more annoyed or anything… well, I might get bothered if I’m interrupted, but mostly it’s just this constant activity, like I’m still supposed to be working.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting several days — four days or more — where you felt distinctly more upbeat, unusually energetic, or unusually irritable, along with a strong drive to keep doing things, even if they weren’t necessary?\n\nPatient: Four days or more… I don’t know if I’d put it like “upbeat” or something. It’s not like I suddenly feel fantastic.\n\nWhat brings me in is more that since I stopped working, I just can’t seem to slow down. I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. I’ll see something that needs fixing, then that turns into another thing, and then I’m halfway into some project I didn’t even mean to start.\n\nSo it’s a strong drive to keep doing things, yeah, but not because I feel unusually happy or anything. It’s just… I don’t seem to shut off.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting at least four days where you felt distinctly more irritable or unusually 'up' in mood, along with a strong drive to keep doing things, even if they weren’t necessary?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it out like that. I’m up and moving all day, going from one little job to the next, like there’s still a crew waiting on me or something.\n\nI’ll fix one thing, then I notice another, then I’m out in the garage, then back in the house—actually, that garage has needed sorting for years, but anyway… it’s like I don’t know how to just sit still now that I’m not working.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed needing less sleep lately, or feeling rested even if you haven’t slept much?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say I need less sleep, no. I mean, I’m not keeping track of it like some people do with those watches—my neighbor has one, tells him everything, even when to breathe practically.\n\nBut… no, I don’t think I’m suddenly fine on hardly any sleep. Some nights are better than others, I guess, but I haven’t noticed feeling unusually rested with very little sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling unusually confident or upbeat lately, maybe more than usual, even to the point where others might notice a change in your mood?\n\nPatient: Unusually confident? I don’t think so. I mean, upbeat… some days I’m in a decent mood, sure, especially if I get something done around the house. Like yesterday I fixed a little latch that’s been bothering me—well, it wasn’t even the latch, it was the wood around it—but no, I don’t think people would say I’m suddenly different or anything.\n\nMy wife might say I’m hard to pin down, but that’s not really the same as being overly confident, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down, sad, or uninterested in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with family?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m down exactly. I mean, I have slow days like anybody, especially when the weather’s bad—rain messes with my knees a bit, and then I end up watching those old house repair shows, which always make everything look easier than it is.\n\nBut sad, or not wanting to be around family… no, I don’t think that’s really it. I still like seeing the kids and grandkids when they come by. Hobbies, well, I bounce around between things, but I don’t think I’ve lost interest so much as I get sidetracked.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was noticeably different—like feeling unusually irritable or on edge, or maybe more energetic or restless than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… restless might be the word more than mood, I guess. I don’t think I’m madder than usual or anything, but since I stopped working, I just can’t seem to slow down.\n\nI’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me or a foreman checking the clock. I’ll go to fix one thing, then notice something else, then I’m in the garage looking for a tool I didn’t even need in the first place. So, yeah, more on edge in the sense that sitting still feels… I don’t know, unnatural.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods recently where you felt a sudden and noticeable change in your energy or mood—like feeling unusually wired, extremely productive, or unable to slow down, even if just for a few days?\n\nPatient: Not like a sudden mood thing, no. I’m up and moving all day, going from one little job to the next like somebody’s waiting on me to finish.\n\nI’ll start with, say, tightening something in the kitchen, then I remember the garage shelf is crooked, then I’m out there moving boxes around—actually I found an old coffee can full of screws the other day, from back when I was doing more work with my hands—and before I know it, the whole morning’s gone.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it “wired,” exactly. It’s just hard for me to slow down.\n\nDoctor: Have there been a time or times when you felt really high, unusually energetic, or extremely productive for several days in a row—like you didn’t need to rest or couldn’t slow down, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “really high,” exactly. I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. Like yesterday I fixed a little latch that’s been bothering me—well, it wasn’t even the latch, it was the wood around it—but no, I don’t think people would say I’m suddenly different or anything.\n\nMy wife might say I’m hard to pin down, but that’s not really the same as being overly confident, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down, sad, or uninterested in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with family?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m down exactly. I mean, I have slow days like anybody, especially when the weather’s bad—rain messes with my knees a bit, and then I end up watching those old house repair shows, which always make everything look easier than it is.\n\nBut sad, or not wanting to be around family… no, I don’t think that’s really it. I still like seeing the kids and grandkids when they come by. Hobbies, well, I bounce around between things, but I don’t think I’ve lost interest so much as I get sidetracked.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was noticeably different—like feeling unusually irritable or on edge, or maybe more energetic or restless than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… restless might be the word more than mood, I guess. I don’t think I’m madder than usual or anything, but since I stopped working, I just can’t seem to slow down.\n\nI’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me or a foreman checking the clock. I’ll go to fix one thing, then notice something else, then I’m in the garage looking for a tool I didn’t even need in the first place. So, yeah, more on edge in the sense that sitting still feels… I don’t know, unnatural.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods recently where you felt a sudden and noticeable change in your energy or mood—like feeling unusually wired, extremely productive, or unable to slow down, even if just for a few days?\n\nPatient: Not like a sudden mood thing, no. It’s more… since I stopped working, I just don’t seem to have an off switch. I’m up and moving all day, going from one little job to the next like somebody’s waiting on me to finish.\n\nI’ll start with, say, tightening something in the kitchen, then I remember the garage shelf is crooked, then I’m out there moving boxes around—actually I found an old coffee can full of screws the other day, from back when I was doing more work with my hands—and before I know it, the whole morning’s gone.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it “wired,” exactly. It’s just hard for me to slow down.\n\nDoctor: Have there been a time or times when you felt really high, unusually energetic, or extremely productive for several days in a row—like you didn’t need to rest or couldn’t slow down, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “really high,” exactly. It’s not like I’m celebrating or feeling on top of the world.\n\nIt’s more that since I stopped working, I just can’t seem to slow down. I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. I’ll finish one thing and then spot another, and then another, and before long I haven’t really sat down much at all. So… energetic, I guess, but not in a special burst for a few days. More like I’ve still got work mode stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt your mood was not just restless, but clearly elevated or unusually irritable—like you were on edge in a different way, maybe more easily annoyed or unusually 'up'—along with feeling driven to keep going and doing things more than usual, even for a few days in a row?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “up” like in a happy way, or clearly different mood-wise. Maybe on edge, but more like I can’t settle.\n\nWhat brings me in is really that since I stopped working, I can’t seem to slow down. I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. I’ll start doing one thing, then I notice something else that needs doing, and pretty soon I’ve been at it for hours.\n\nIt’s not so much that I’m more annoyed or anything… well, I might get bothered if I’m interrupted, but mostly it’s just this constant activity, like I’m still supposed to be working.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting several days — four days or more — where you felt distinctly more upbeat, unusually energetic, or unusually irritable, along with a strong drive to keep doing things, even if they weren’t necessary?\n\nPatient: Four days or more… I don’t know if I’d put it like “upbeat” or something. It’s not like I suddenly feel fantastic.\n\nWhat brings me in is more that since I stopped working, I just can’t seem to slow down. I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. I’ll see something that needs fixing, then that turns into another thing, and then I’m halfway into some project I didn’t even mean to start.\n\nSo it’s a strong drive to keep doing things, yeah, but not because I feel unusually happy or anything. It’s just… I don’t seem to shut off.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting at least four days where you felt distinctly more irritable or unusually 'up' in mood, along with a strong drive to keep doing things, even if they weren’t necessary?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it out like that. I don’t really remember a stretch where my mood was “up,” like unusually good, for four days or anything.\n\nI do get going on things and have trouble stopping, but that feels more like… well, if I see the garage needs sweeping, then I notice the shelf is crooked, then I’m looking for a tool I put somewhere—actually I still can’t find my good tape measure, and that drives me nuts. But mood-wise, I don’t know. I haven’t noticed a clear few days where I was different like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting at least four days where your mood was noticeably different, like feeling unusually irritable or unusually 'up' or high, where it was clear to you or others that something was off in your mood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not in a way where someone would say, “What’s going on with you?” mood-wise.\n\nI mean, I can get wrapped up in doing things, and then my wife might ask why I’m messing with something that doesn’t need fixing right then, but that’s more about me keeping busy. As far as feeling high or unusually irritable for several days… I haven’t really noticed that. I’m not sure I’d recognize it unless somebody pointed it out, but no, nothing clear comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where your mood was clearly different from usual—like feeling unusually excited, overly confident, or easily annoyed for several days in a row—even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of anything like that… excited or overly confident for days, no, I don’t really recognize that in myself.\n\nEasily annoyed, sure, I can get annoyed—like if I’m looking for something and it’s not where I swear I left it, or someone moves a tool and doesn’t put it back. Actually, my toolbox is a mess right now, I’ve been meaning to sort it, but then I start doing that and find something else that needs doing.\n\nBut as far as a clear stretch of several days where my mood was different from normal, I can’t really point to one. Nothing that stands out that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where your mood was clearly different from usual—like feeling unusually excited, overly confident, or easily annoyed—lasting at least four days, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had that, not clearly. I mean, I can be cranky for a bit if something’s bothering me, but four days where my mood is really different from normal… I can’t point to anything like that.\n\nSometimes I’ll get caught up doing one thing after another—like yesterday I went to put one box away and ended up reorganizing half a shelf, then found some old screws I forgot I had—but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually excited or confident or anything. Nothing that stands out that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where your mood was clearly different from usual—like feeling unusually excited, overly confident, or easily annoyed—lasting at least four days, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had that, not clearly. No, I don’t think so. I know you’ve asked it a couple ways, and I’m trying not to miss something, but I can’t think of a stretch like that—four days or more where my mood was clearly “off” or where people noticed I was acting unusually high or overly confident.

I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. It’s just… I don’t seem to shut off.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting at least four days where you felt distinctly more irritable or unusually 'up' in mood, along with a strong drive to keep doing things, even if they weren’t necessary?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it out like that. I’m up and moving all day, going from one little job to the next like somebody’s waiting on me to finish.\n\nI’ll start with, say, tightening something in the kitchen, then I remember the garage shelf is crooked, then I’m out there moving boxes around—actually I found an old coffee can full of screws the other day, from back when I was doing more work with my hands—and before I know it, the whole morning’s gone.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it “wired,” exactly. It’s just hard for me to slow down.\n\nDoctor: Have there been a time or times when you felt really high, unusually energetic, or extremely productive for several days in a row—like you didn’t need to rest or couldn’t slow down, even if it didn’t last very long?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “really high,” exactly. It’s not like I’m celebrating or feeling on top of the world.\n\nIt’s more that since I stopped working, I just can’t seem to slow down. I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. I’ll finish one thing and then spot another, and then another, and before long I haven’t really sat down much at all. So… energetic, I guess, but not in a special burst for a few days. More like I’ve still got work mode stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt your mood was not just restless, but clearly elevated or unusually irritable—like you were on edge in a different way, maybe more easily annoyed or unusually 'up'—along with feeling driven to keep going and doing things more than usual, even for a few days in a row?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “up” like in a happy way, or clearly different mood-wise. I’m up and moving from one little job to the next all day, like I still have a crew waiting on me. I’ll start doing one thing, then I notice something else that needs doing, and pretty soon I’ve been at it for hours.\n\nIt’s not so much that I’m more annoyed or anything… well, I might get bothered if I’m interrupted, but mostly it’s just this constant activity, like I’m still supposed to be working.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods lasting several days — four days or more — where you felt distinctly more upbeat, unusually energetic, or unusually irritable, along with a strong drive to keep doing things, even if they weren’t necessary?\n\nPatient: Four days or more… I don’t know if I’d put it like “upbeat” or something. I don’t think so… not for four days like that.
